# Components

What this project can already do, by capability. Search it before writing new code:

```bash
python3 scripts/components_index.py search <what you need, in plain words>
```

Each entry is written by hand (`does`, `verbs`, `status`, `modules`, `tests`). The
`uses` / `used_by` lines and the verbs list are generated by `python3 scripts/components_index.py write`.

Add one entry per component below this line, in this form (without the indentation):

    ### area.name
    - does: One sentence saying what it is for
    - verbs: phrase people would search for, another phrase
    - status: canonical
    - modules: src/conference_pipeline/<file>.py
    - tests: tests/<test file>.py

## Subsystems

### subsystem.workflow_run
- does: Run selected pipeline stages for one conference workspace in live, dry-run or mock mode from the conference-pipeline CLI, with receipts and lineage
- verbs: conference-pipeline workflow run, run workflow, run stages, dry run, mock run, workflow receipts, resume workflow, stage status, bind lineage
- status: canonical
- components: cli.conference_pipeline, workflow.stage_catalog, workflow.runs, workflow.lineage, llm.provider_receipts, workflow.contract
- members_uses: acquisition.conference_site_auth, acquisition.event_media, acquisition.public_material, acquisition.registration_media, acquisition.youtube_talk_harvest, analysis.manifest, benchmark.local_promotion, conference.cvpr2026_r5_transcription_ocr, conference.cvpr2026_r6_artifact_contract, conference.cvpr2026_r6_material_index, conference.cvpr2026_r6_recovery_inventory, conference.iclr2025_live, … +25 more (graph.md)
- flow: cli.conference_pipeline -> workflow.stage_catalog -> workflow.runs -> workflow.lineage -> llm.provider_receipts -> workflow.contract
- notes: The CLI selects stages from the catalog first (cli.py:288). The runner binds lineage before a stage executes (workflow_runs.py:482), runs the stage inside a provider-receipt context (:637, :865) and collects its provider usage into the stage receipt (:1225), then verifies the stage contract (:735, :968) only for a live, succeeded receipt (:1292). Per-conference production runs go through subsystem.production_run instead.

### subsystem.production_run
- does: Run one live production stage (01-12) per invocation through a conference's thin runner, the shared orchestrator, stage table and stage modules
- verbs: production run, run production stages, conference runner, stage table, stages 01-12, preflight, source discovery, corpus freeze, download corpus, transcription ocr, corpus index, release, final proof
- status: canonical
- components: conference.cvpr2026_runner, production.orchestrator, production.stage_table, production.runner_context, production.stage_receipts, production.preflight_stage, production.source_discovery_stage, production.corpus_freeze_stage, production.download_corpus_stage, production.transcription_ocr_stage, production.corpus_index_stage, production.exhaustive_extraction_stage, production.analysis_stage, production.qa_synthesis_stage, production.podcast_stage, production.presentation_stage, production.notebooklm_podcast_stage, production.release_stage, production.proof_stage
- members_uses: acquisition.event_media, acquisition.openreview_auth, acquisition.openreview_workshops, acquisition.organizer_channel_talk_lane, acquisition.proceedings_tracks, acquisition.program_item_lane, acquisition.program_spine, acquisition.registration_media, acquisition.source_discovery, acquisition.talk_gap_search, acquisition.workshop_paper_registry, acquisition.youtube_talk_harvest, … +33 more (graph.md)
- flow: conference.cvpr2026_runner -> production.orchestrator -> production.stage_table -> production.runner_context -> production.stage_receipts
- notes: One invocation runs one stage: orchestrator.dispatch looks up the handler for --stage and calls only that one (production/orchestrator.py:260-274); the operator runs stages 01-12 in stage-table order, each resuming from the earlier stages' receipts. The stage modules are listed in components in stage-table order (stage_owner_registry.json stage_order): podcast is 9, presentation and the NotebookLM podcast are both 10, and presentation requires the stage 9 receipts (production/presentation_stage.py:145-154). CVPR 2026's runner is the reference; conference.icml2026_runner and conference.iclr2026_runner drive the same stages with their own hooks.

### subsystem.presentation_build
- does: Build one topic deck end to end inside the production presentation stage: blueprint, figures, render, visual QC, repair, Gate A review, narration, video
- verbs: build presentation, build deck, presentation chain, deck chain, topic deck, blueprint to video, render deck, visual qc, gate a review, narrated video
- status: canonical
- components: production.presentation_stage, production.presentation_chain, production.presentation_steps, workflow.deck_step_lineage, presentation.stage_commands, presentation.advisory_visual_qc
- members_uses: figures.operator_findings, figures.regression, indexing.corpus_vector_store, llm.model_registry, llm.provider_receipts, matching.paper_matching, presentation.blueprint_contract, presentation.legacy_markdown_gate, presentation.manifest, presentation.review_settings, production.deck_scope, production.runner_context, … +5 more (graph.md)
- flow: production.presentation_stage -> production.presentation_chain -> production.presentation_steps -> workflow.deck_step_lineage -> presentation.stage_commands -> presentation.advisory_visual_qc
- notes: Most chain steps run owner scripts under scripts/presentations/ as subprocesses (blueprint generation, figure compile, render, QC, review, narration, video), so they are not import edges; the step order is in production/presentation_chain.py.

### subsystem.public_release
- does: Publish one conference workspace's public release: export gate, bundle, dataset package, search index, static site, public repo, CDN, live deployment
- verbs: public release, publish conference, release to public, export bundle, hugging face dataset, zenodo, static site, github pages, public repo, cdn upload, closeout
- status: canonical
- components: publishing.release_cli, publishing.release_manifest_draft, publishing.release_cli_wrappers, publishing.release_orchestration, publishing.export_gate, publishing.public_index, publishing.static_site, publishing.public_repo, publishing.dataset_package, publishing.cdn, publishing.public_export, publishing.live_deployment, publishing.release_layout, publishing.surface_limits, publishing.search_shards, publishing.embedding_cache, publishing.release_hub
- members_uses: indexing.embedding_selection, llm.model_registry, publishing.bundle_redaction, publishing.byok_tools, publishing.hub_site, publishing.notebooklm_audio, publishing.reader_bundle, publishing.reader_families, publishing.receipt_path_confinement, publishing.release_licenses, publishing.self_host_kit, publishing.site_checks, … +1 more (graph.md)
- flow: publishing.release_cli -> publishing.release_orchestration -> publishing.export_gate -> publishing.public_index -> publishing.static_site -> publishing.public_repo -> publishing.dataset_package -> publishing.cdn
- notes: Entry point scripts/publish_conference_public_release.py; each step is a gated child script in the release journal (public_release.py:440-720): export (publishing.public_export), index, site, repo staging, dataset packaging, deployment preflight (publishing.live_deployment), URL and metadata checks, then the live pushes (repo, media upload, Pages, Hugging Face, Zenodo). The closeout record is a later separate step (publishing.closeout).

### subsystem.drive_backup
- does: Back up conference workspace artifacts to Google Drive on a schedule, by the scheduled sweep driving the Drive sync script
- verbs: drive backup, google drive backup, backup sweep, scheduled backup, sync to drive, upload to google drive, rclone
- status: canonical
- components: storage.backup_sweep, storage.drive_sync
- members_uses: (none)
- flow: storage.backup_sweep -> storage.drive_sync
- notes: The sweep runs scripts/sync_to_gdrive.py as a subprocess, so the step is not an import edge.

### subsystem.control_plane
- does: Local operator web app that launches workflow runs and the smoke-test preset and previews their artifacts
- verbs: control plane, operator ui, web app, launch run, run status, artifact preview, smoke test preset
- status: canonical
- components: ui.control_plane, ui.run_manager, workflow.runs, workflow.smoke_test, ui.artifact_preview
- members_uses: analysis.manifest, costs.budget_enforcement, costs.rollup, extraction.local_llm_backends, extraction.topic_classifier, llm.provider_receipts, llm.vision_lane_status, presentation.structural_validation, workflow.contract, workflow.lineage, workflow.stage_catalog, workflow.stage_script_registry
- flow: ui.control_plane -> ui.run_manager -> workflow.runs
- notes: Two alternative launches: run_manager.launch runs workflow stages, launch_preset runs the smoke-test preset (workflow.smoke_test) instead. Artifact previews (ui.artifact_preview) are separate routes in control_plane/app.py, not a step after a run.

### production.paper_admission
- does: Admits a workspace's late paper sets into a Stage-6-format corpus inventory by stable identity, with every existing record kept, appends its transcript-lane talks and their named gaps, and writes the identity report and the corpus statistics (papers, artifacts, text coverage, talks by kind, corpus version) [admit_paper_sets]
- verbs: admit late papers, fold in papers, merge corpus, paper total union of ids, text coverage before after, papers gained text, corpus statistics, corpus version label, previous corpus, papers admit, recompute statistics, idempotent admission
- status: canonical
- modules: src/conference_pipeline/production/paper_admission.py, src/conference_pipeline/production/paper_admission_cli.py
- uses: indexing.paper_identity, indexing.paper_lanes, indexing.transcript_evidence_lanes, production.corpus_index_stage, publishing.receipt_path_confinement
- used_by: cli.conference_pipeline
- tests: tests/test_paper_admission.py
- notes: v6.0 Phase 5. Reuses corpus_index_stage.make_corpus_record and artifact_identity. It does not call curate_papers, whose 5,000-byte image-only rule would drop every abstract (spec IF2); it keeps one record per paper_key by the same ordering and marks the rest duplicate_copy. CLI: conference-pipeline papers admit <slug> [--dry-run] [--project-root]. v6.0 Phase 5.1: when storage/transcription-ocr/evidence_lanes.json exists, lane records are appended after the paper tallies are computed, so a paper-only workspace's outputs are byte-identical to Phase 5's.

- does: The conference-pipeline release command group: init drafts the export manifest (or --refresh adds missing storage families), families builds the reader families, plan dry-runs, build writes the bundle and derived stages, check re-verifies (including each surface's provider limits), publish pushes with the owner's named approval (github, r2, pages, hf, zenodo) after the same limit check; isolated rebuilds for comparison; release hub build/check/publish (release_hub) [release_init, release_plan, release_build, release_check, release_publish]
- verbs: release command, one command release, conference-pipeline release, release init, release plan, release build, release check, release publish, approved by, owner approval, rebuild published bundle, isolated rebuild, reproduce bundle digest, publish surface, release exit codes, release targets config
- status: canonical
- modules: src/conference_pipeline/publishing/release_cli.py
- uses: publishing.export_gate, publishing.reader_families, publishing.receipt_path_confinement, publishing.release_hub, publishing.release_layout, publishing.release_licenses, publishing.release_manifest_draft, publishing.release_orchestration, publishing.surface_limits, security.private_markers
- used_by: cli.conference_pipeline
- part_of: subsystem.public_release
- tests: tests/test_release_cli.py
- notes: v6.0 Phase 3. Registered on the CLI by cli.py (app.add_typer(release_app, name="release")). Exit codes 0 done, 1 check finding or isolated digest mismatch, 2 refused. Each command writes its own top-level receipt public-release-<bundle>-<command>-receipt.json. --isolated writes nothing under the workspace and skips the export's immutability guard (it never touches the published bundle root).

### publishing.pdf_regeneration
- does: Renders each deck's published HTML (after its payload family's redaction rules: relink, inline_assets) to PDF once, from a temporary folder, for the reader-families staging folder; a page that still loads any local resource is refused, early when an HTML parser finds it (sources, srcset, link, SVG image or use, CSS url and @import, an iframe's srcdoc) and always when the renderer, which loads only the page, https, data, blob and about URLs, refused a load, the PDF's render dates are pinned so a regeneration gives the same bytes, and a PDF that still holds a publishing marker is excluded and reported [regenerate_deck_pdfs]
- verbs: regenerate pdf, deck pdf from published html, pdf without drive links, binary marker fix, d8 regenerate, release pdfs, staged pdfs
- status: canonical
- modules: src/conference_pipeline/publishing/pdf_regeneration.py
- uses: presentation.render_spec_export, publishing.bundle_redaction, security.private_markers
- used_by: publishing.reader_families
- tests: tests/test_release_reader_families.py
- notes: v6.0 Phase 6b (ruling D8 a). Declared as regenerated_pdfs in storage/release/reader_sources.json; output pdfs/<deck>/presentation.pdf, published by a staging family. Rendered once so isolated builds stay identical (a fresh Playwright PDF differs in timestamps). Refuses HTML that still loads a local image (pdf_html_not_self_contained). Never writes the workspace.

### publishing.reader_families
- does: Builds a release's reader families into storage/release-staging/<bundle>: catalog rows by format adapter (validated against the catalog row schema), topic papers, excluded ids, statistics with display-named talks, narratives, and a limitations record whose every quote must sit at its cited close-record lines; checks the record row by row [build_reader_families, check_limitations, talk_display_name]
- verbs: paper catalog, catalog rows, papers jsonl, official url, topic papers, excluded ids, corpus statistics for a release, talks by kind, talk display names, narratives for release, limitations record, accepted findings, close record, row by row check, release families, d11 note, talk transcripts for release, transcript index
- status: canonical
- modules: src/conference_pipeline/publishing/reader_families.py
- uses: indexing.corpus_vector_store, publishing.pdf_regeneration, security.private_markers
- used_by: publishing.release_cli, publishing.static_site
- tests: tests/test_release_reader_families.py
- notes: v6.0 Phase 6 (6a; regenerated_pdfs, presentations and transcripts 6b, rulings D8 a, D22, D23: presentations stages a release presentation manifest listing every deck with published HTML, titled from its frame; transcripts stages each admitted talk transcript, read through Stage 6's read_indexable_source_text and made plain (captions, JSON, annotated recordings), as transcripts/<item_id or video-id>.md with a header and notice, plus transcripts/index.jsonl). stage6_inventory also identifies papers from per-paper download receipts, a record's own source_url, or the download corpus (two papers under one receipt id or one paper id are refused; one paper under two receipts is one row), and maps storage lanes to tracks (CVPR 2026). Sources are declared per workspace in storage/release/reader_sources.json; adapters are named by format (corpus_inventory, openreview_accepted_papers). A refusal (ReaderFamiliesError) writes nothing. CLI: conference-pipeline release families <slug>. presentations may title decks from a Markdown heading (title_markdown); transcript ids from namespaced video ids are made file-safe (6b).

### publishing.bundle_redaction
- does: Redacts a file's bundle copy by a family's redaction_rules objects (json_field, absolute_path, relink through a link map incl. bare Drive URLs, marker_text, inline_assets: local images become data: URIs, always last); never rewrites a binary, only reports one holding a marker [apply_redaction_rules, project_root_of]
- verbs: redact bundle copy, scrub policy, private target redaction, relink drive links, absolute paths to relative, redaction rules, binary markers, bundle field rules, inline images, data uri, self-contained html, bare drive url
- status: canonical
- modules: src/conference_pipeline/publishing/bundle_redaction.py
- uses: security.private_markers
- used_by: publishing.pdf_regeneration, publishing.public_export
- tests: tests/test_release_reader_families.py
- notes: v6.0 Phase 6 (6a; bare URLs and inline_assets 6b, ruling D21). inline_assets needs source_path and refuses an image outside the project, through a link, missing, or holding a marker. Called by scripts/export_for_public.py while planning, so the planned digest, the written bytes and CHECKSUMS.json all describe the redacted copy; the receipt lists redacted_files and binary_markers. String rules are legacy names and do nothing. pdf_links removes non-public link annotations and drops the orphaned objects, so a removed link's target is not left in the file (6b review r3). figure_links (6b, ruling D25) replaces each local figure in deck Markdown with a line naming its paper from the deck's asset_inventory.json, linked through the release catalog by title.

- verbs: draft export manifest, release init, public export manifest draft, excluded families, promote family, storage manifest families, default excluded, manifest from storage manifest

### publishing.site_checks
- does: Checks a built site or hub: links resolve to site files or https URLs, an offline accessibility lint, a Playwright mobile render; plus the BM25 reference ranking (search_text reads a site's sharded index the way the browser does) and the narrative HTML sanitizer, which also names repeated link texts with aria-label (6b) [check_links, lint_accessibility, render_mobile, bm25_rank, search_text, sanitize_html, render_markdown]
- verbs: link check, accessibility lint, wcag check, mobile render, horizontal overflow, playwright screenshot check, bm25 search, sanitize html, render markdown narrative

- uses: publishing.search_shards
- used_by: publishing.release_hub, publishing.search_shards, publishing.static_site
- tests: tests/test_site_checks.py
- notes: The render follows presentation.rendered_qc's pattern: a missing Playwright or Chromium is a named skip, never a pass. render_markdown levels headings so none skips a level, and takes reserved page link texts; the link lint reads aria-label first (6b).

## storage

### security.private_markers
- does: Find private markers (home paths, Drive accounts, cloud mounts, real emails, shell-profile secret reads) in a tree, redacted; a published file's bytes are checked whole, UTF-8 or UTF-16 text by every rule, a PDF by its extracted text, its bytes outside its streams and every object it holds (each string, each stream decoded, referenced or not) and a zip by its member names, member comments and members, UTF-16 text in any bytes holding a NUL by every rule (four decoded views; .env on the bytes), binary bytes by the literal tokens and the operator's extra markers, and a PDF that is encrypted or holds a stream, or a zip a member, that cannot be read whole fails closed [scan_paths]
- verbs: private marker scan, find home path, scrub private data, detect personal email, drive account path, cloud storage path, shell profile secret, zshrc api key, redact findings, public readiness, privacy audit, allowlist exemptions, operator identifiers, leak check, scan before publishing, pre-publish scan, scan binary file for markers, publishing marker bytes, pdf drive link check
- status: canonical
- modules: src/conference_pipeline/_security/private_markers.py
- uses: (none)
- used_by: audit.private_markers_cli, publishing.bundle_redaction, publishing.byok_tools, publishing.closeout, publishing.code_export, publishing.dataset_package, publishing.notebooklm_audio, publishing.pdf_regeneration, publishing.public_index, publishing.public_repo, publishing.reader_families, publishing.release_cli, … +3 more (graph.md)
- tests: tests/test_private_markers.py
- notes: v6.0 Phase 6: publishing_literal_rules() turns the literal publishing tokens into rules, so release check refuses what the site build refuses. Generic rules only; the operator's own literals come from CONFERENCE_PIPELINE_PRIVATE_MARKERS at run time and are never printed. Policy: docs/operations/public-release-scrub-policy.md. Since v6.0 Phase 3 the publishing gates share publishing_marker_hits / has_publishing_marker / redact_publishing_markers: the legacy literal tokens unioned with these rules. has_publishing_marker_bytes also opens zip containers (.pptx, .docx) and scans each member, failing closed on an unreadable archive; a /home/ or /Users/ segment inside an http(s) URL path is not a home path (6b). ".env" has one file-name rule (ENV_FILE_NAME: the token unless a letter or digit continues it) for every scan and file type, text, UTF-16, PDF and zip members alike; only opaque binary or compressed bytes also need a path separator before it. No gate skips .env findings for PDFs or zips (PR 49, after pr-49-r6). A PDF or zip has one verdict in every gate, this check's: a PDF's text outside its streams (comments, object syntax; each stream cut out at the place the parser reads it from its cross-reference offset) and a zip's member names and comments meet every rule, and UTF-16 text in any bytes meets every rule, whatever the encoding guess: four decoded views (either byte order, either alignment) for every rule but .env, which is read on the bytes in its UTF-16 form so a view read at the wrong alignment cannot invent the end of a word (pr-49-r7, r8).
